# Plan — Optum Market Clarity / Enriched Oncology feasibility & best-practice work

**Sponsor context:** GSK oncology epidemiology / RWE, evaluating whether Optum **Market Clarity** (linked EHR + claims) plus the **Enriched Oncology** NLP tables can support specific studies — Dostarlimab **JADE** (HNSCC safety), **AZUR‑2** (treatment-phase contribution / advanced CRC), H&N **RDQ 316079** (HPV, smoking/alcohol), and advanced-CRC trial-like-population RDQs.

**Engagement scope (confirmed):** *documentation now, data later.* Build everything from the data dictionary so it is **executable once data access lands** — i.e. designed to run after the per-cohort source placeholders are wired and the SQL dialect is validated (see §7 / `docs/review-notes.md`), not runnable as-is today.

**Deliverables:** (1) best-practice playbook · (2) question→table mapping · (3) case-study **worked examples** · (4) variable-availability matrix · (5) **runnable coverage profiling** (Databricks `notebooks/`) that populates the matrix. The full six-cohort pipeline is an optional appendix (`sql/reference_pipeline/`).

See **`docs/data-model-reference.md`** for the consolidated factual reference (tables, keys, code systems, NLP performance, methodology rules) that every deliverable builds on.

---

## 1. What was reviewed

| File | What it is | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `Questions to Answer/optum mc.pdf` | GSK deck: "Best Practice Guidance / Epi needs" + 2 case studies + priority-tumor list + a variable-availability matrix template | **Defines the asks** |
| `Questions to Answer/Sample Query.pdf` | "Market Clarity enhanced Oncology use-cases" — the driving studies (JADE, AZUR‑2, RDQ 316079) + overarching questions | **Defines the why** |
| `Optum Data Dict/optum onco training decl.pdf` | Optum NLP & Enriched Oncology training deck (46 slides): schema, table walkthrough, samples | **Describes the data** |
| `Optum Data Dict/optum enriched oncology manual.pdf` | Enriched Oncology User Guide (27 pp.): methodology, rules, LoT algorithm, appendix SQL + ICD/biomarker lists | **Describes the method** |
| `Optum Data Dict/optum data location.pdf` | Catalog Explorer screenshots of the **deployed** location (host, catalog `hive_metastore`, schema `clnprw_optum_mc_full_use`, cut `2025q4`, the 52 `t_*_2025q4` tables) | **Pins the real schema** |
| `Optum Data Dict/optum data dict/` (contents + part 1/2) | Column-level data dictionary (table definitions + per-table columns) | **Confirms columns** |
| `Optum Data Dict/optum entity relationship.pdf` | Entity-relationship diagram | **Confirms keys/joins** |

The first four are image-based PDFs (rendered to images and read page-by-page); the data-location + column dictionary were added later and used to re-point the notebooks to the deployed schema (rounds 22–24).

---

## 2. The asks, decomposed

**A. Best-practice / "Epi needs" methodology questions** → *Playbook*
Patient identification (ICD‑10 + histological confirmation) · outcomes (PFS / ORR / OS via mortality linkage) · staging (TNM / FIGO) · response & progression data · SDS / NLP-derived elements · continuum of care (IDN flag + claims linkage) · LoT rules · oral / maintenance / surgery / neoadjuvant / adjuvant · tokenization & vendors · EHR-vs-claims discordance · table-vs-table discordance · structured vs unstructured · biomarkers (+ LOINC) · HPV derivation · smoking / alcohol completeness · single source of truth.

**B. Case Study A — dMMR/MSI‑H locally advanced colon cancer** → *Cohort spec A*
6-step attrition, run across **6 data-source definitions** (EHR only; EHR + Optum claims ×2; EHR + third-party claims ×2; Market Clarity enriched):
1. colon-cancer surgery → 2. newly diagnosed ≤183 d before surgery → 3. stage T1‑4N+ or T4N0 (else lymph nodes >0) → 4. no metastatic dx before surgery → 5. dMMR+/MSI‑H → 6. IO use after dx and before metastatic dx.
Plus: feasibility commentary on effectiveness / tolerability / natural-history outcomes; longitudinality, completeness, accuracy, validatability; **cohort overlap** across sources.

**C. Case Study B — Ovarian cancer** → *Cohort spec B*
Counts/proportions: total OC; by LoT (neoadjuvant, adjuvant, 1L, 2L+); 2L+ stratified **platinum-sensitive vs -resistant** (progression >6 mo vs ≤6 mo after platinum LoT end). Characteristics (feasibility): age at dx, FIGO stage, histology, race/ethnicity, BMI, ECOG, primary cytoreductive surgery (Y/N). Same 6-source overlap framing.

**D. Priority-tumour counts** → folded into the matrix / a counts query
Ovarian, Endometrial, Colorectal, Prostate, Multiple myeloma, NSCLC, HNSCC, Sarcoma, Bladder (+ broader Gyn/GI/GU/Heme/Lung/Other list).

**E. Variable-availability matrix** → *Matrix deliverable* (with **two rows per variable**: off-the-shelf oncology data vs curation/derived, per GSK's note).

---

## 3. Key findings that shape the answers

1. **There is no single "source of truth" for staging — by design.** Stage/severity/surgery span Stage, TNM, Characteristics, Grade, Metastasis Location, Evaluation System + structured Diagnosis/Procedure. Optum says combine them. → The playbook will give an explicit **precedence/triangulation hierarchy** rather than pick one table. *(Directly answers GSK's "single source of truth / correlation between tables.")*

2. **Moderate NLP recall (0.6–0.8) → systematic undercapture; absence ≠ negative.** This is the mechanism behind the **<1% HPV** and **~50% smoking** observations. → For HPV (RDQ 316079): **triangulate** Biomarker table + structured **Labs/LOINC** + diagnosis/procedure codes, and report capture as a floor, not a true rate.

3. **Smoking / alcohol are not in the `onc_*` tables at all** — they sit in **NLP SDS / Observations / Measurement** (+ structured social history). → Re-point the RDQ‑316079 analysis to those tables; the ~50%/high-missingness pattern is expected from provider-activity-driven capture.

4. **The Line-of-Therapy table is IV-only and excludes oral chemo + has no neoadjuvant/adjuvant flag.** → For ovarian (oral/maintenance, neoadj/adj, platinum lines) we must build a **custom LoT** from the **Oncology Medications** table (which includes oral + claims) and derive neoadj/adj from **timing relative to surgery**. Platinum-sensitivity = progression timing vs platinum-LoT end. *(Answers "oral therapies? maintenance? neoadjuvant? adjuvant?")*

5. **Outcomes are derivable with caveats:** ORR ≈ Treatment Response; **PFS** = `onc_progression` (`progression IN ('progression','recurrence')`, current; date = `note_date`) + treatment dates (no native field); **OS** uses **`patient.date_of_death`** (`YYYYMM`) + `deceased_indicator` — **source confirmed**; open item is completeness/lag/fitness for OS (month-only granularity).

6. **Tokenization is not addressed anywhere in the provided docs.** → Flagged as an **open question for Optum** (the common vendor is Datavant); cannot be answered from these materials.

7. **dMMR/MSI‑H has a clean derivation path:** mutation in any of **MLH1/MSH2/MSH6/PMS2** (Biomarker table) ∪ MSI‑H result, cross-checked against structured Labs. → Case Study A step 5 is feasible.

8. **The manual appendix ships reusable SQL + the Cancer ICD‑9/10 inclusion list + biomarker labs list** — the backbone for patient identification and the case-study query skeletons.

---

## 4. Phased plan

> Phases 0–3 are fully **doc-derivable now**; Phase 4 executes when data access lands. The SQL is
> parameterized to run **once schema-mapped and dialect-validated** (documented Optum schema/table names;
> a few non-§4.8.2 table spellings to confirm) — not literally unchanged.

**Phase 0 — Foundation** ✅ *(this commit)*
`docs/data-model-reference.md` — consolidated tables/keys/codes/methodology/performance.

**Phase 1 — Playbook + Mapping** ✅ *(done)*
- `docs/best-practice-playbook.md` — one section per Epi-needs question, each with: recommended approach, exact tables/fields/codes, derivation logic, caveats, and the relevant NLP-recall caveat.
- `docs/question-to-table-mapping.md` — a row per GSK variable → table(s) → field(s) → derivation → primary/secondary source → confidence/caveat.

**Phase 2 — Case-study WORKED EXAMPLES** ✅ *(done)*
- Per the "use-cases for training" intent, the case studies are presented as **illustrative worked
  examples** (a query snippet per concept + feasibility commentary), not a full pipeline:
  `docs/case_studies/A-colon-dMMR-MSI.md`, `docs/case_studies/B-ovarian.md`.
- The full, parameterized, end-to-end SQL (6-cohort attrition, recursive LoT, value sets) is preserved
  as an **optional appendix** in `sql/reference_pipeline/` — not the primary deliverable.

**Phase 3 — Variable-availability matrix + coverage profiling** ✅ *(done)*
- `docs/variable-availability-matrix.md` (+ `.csv`) — variables × sources, color/legend, off-the-shelf vs curation columns. Includes the priority-tumour counts template.
- `notebooks/` + `docs/coverage-assessment.md` — the **runnable mechanism that populates the matrix**:
  per-variable, per-source coverage; off-the-shelf-vs-curation lift; staging source coverage + overlap;
  and one count per Epi question. This is a first-class primary deliverable (the "what coverage" ask).

**Phase 4 — Execution (on data access)**
Run the `notebooks/` → populate the **direct-variable** coverage %s + the HPV/smoking/tumour-size anchors. The
six-source **attrition Ns, cohort overlaps, and EHR-vs-claims discordance rates are NOT a one-button output** —
they require building the Optum-claims unions, value-concordance queries, and a Spark LoT first (the appendix is
the design; cohorts 3a/3b are not-assessable without a third-party asset). Then → validate → feasibility report.

## 5. Variable-availability matrix — shape (to be filled)

**Rows (variables):** cancer dx / patient ID · histology · age at dx · sex · race/ethnicity · BMI · ECOG/performance status · smoking · alcohol · stage (overall) · TNM · FIGO · grade (Gleason/Nottingham/…) · tumor characteristics · metastasis location · tumor size · lymph nodes · surgical margin · biomarkers (general) · dMMR/MSI‑H · HPV · PD‑L1 · surgery (primary/cytoreductive) · neoadjuvant · adjuvant · LoT (IV) · oral/maintenance therapy · treatment response (ORR) · progression / PFS · mortality / OS · HCRU · comorbidities / safety events · continuous enrollment / observable time.

**Columns (sources):** Structured EHR · Enriched Oncology (off-the-shelf) · Enriched Oncology (curation/derived) · Optum claims · Third-party claims.

**Legend:** captured · partial/low-recall · derivable-via-curation · not captured.

**optional appendix** (`sql/reference_pipeline/`). **Many review rounds** are incorporated — see

deployed catalog `hive_metastore.clnprw_optum_mc_full_use` (cut `2025q4`), fixed the deployed-column mismatches
and Case A/B clinical logic (incl. the Case A ≥2-dx cohort from step 1, assay-specific dMMR, in-window IO),
added a column **preflight**, made 3a/3b not-assessable (`n/a`), and reconciled the docs).

`HOST`/`HTTP_PATH` + the biomarker LOINC value-set + a slice). The **case-study notebooks are simplified demos**;
the **six-source attrition, cohort overlap, and EHR-vs-claims discordance are NOT built end-to-end** — Case A's
2a/2b Optum-claims union isn't wired (3a/3b are not-assessable here), Case B's LoT is gap-only, and the
value-concordance queries don't exist yet. So the appendix's "attrition Ns / overlaps / discordance rates" are a
**design**, not a validated output.

**To produce numbers — the `notebooks/`:** set `HOST`/`HTTP_PATH` (schema/cut already set), supply the biomarker
LOINC value-set, set the cohort in `_config_tumours.py`, let the **preflight** confirm columns, run
`coverage_optum_databricks.py` + `epi_counts_optum_databricks.py` against a test slice, sanity-check the anchors
(HPV <1%, smoking ~50%, tumour size ~6%), then populate the matrix. Depends on the **external** gates in
**`docs/optum-gsk-questions.md`**. Final output shell: `docs/feasibility-report-template.md`.

# optum_mc

Feasibility & best-practice work for using **Optum Market Clarity** (linked EHR + claims) and the
**Enriched Oncology** NLP tables to support GSK oncology RWE studies.

## Source material
- `Questions to Answer/` — GSK decks defining the asks (Epi needs, case studies, driving studies).
- `Optum Data Dict/` — Optum reference docs (NLP & Enriched Oncology training deck; Enriched Oncology user guide).

## Work products
- **[`docs/methodology-and-derivation.md`](docs/methodology-and-derivation.md)** — **stakeholder explainer**:
  how the tumour cohorts, code lists, and coverage approach were derived (what's confirmed from Optum docs
  vs the GSK asks vs illustrative pending sign-off). Start here to explain the work.
- **[`PLAN.md`](PLAN.md)** — review of the source material + the phased plan and deliverables.
- **[`docs/data-model-reference.md`](docs/data-model-reference.md)** — consolidated data-model reference
  (tables, keys, code systems, NLP performance, methodology rules) distilled from the Optum docs.
- **[`docs/best-practice-playbook.md`](docs/best-practice-playbook.md)** — answers GSK's "Epi needs"
  questions (patient ID, staging, biomarkers/HPV, smoking/alcohol, LoT/oral/neoadj-adj, outcomes,
  continuity, discordance, tokenization).
- **[`docs/question-to-table-mapping.md`](docs/question-to-table-mapping.md)** — each variable →
  table(s)/field(s) → derivation → data layer → capture caveat.
- **[`notebooks/`](notebooks/)** — **runnable coverage profiling** as Databricks notebooks (the house
  style: `run_sql` + pandas, Spark SQL, small-cell suppression, CSV/`display`): how many patients actually
  have each variable populated, by source, to fill the "key data variables" matrix and reproduce the
  HPV-<1% / smoking-~50% findings. `coverage_optum_databricks.py` does direct-variable coverage (EO + selected
  structured/claims inputs — **not** the full five-layer matrix: no third-party-claims column, no EHR-vs-claims
  discordance) + off-the-shelf-vs-curated lift + staging overlap; `epi_counts_optum_databricks.py` gives one
  count per Epi question.
  Tumours are config in `_config_tumours.py`. See [`notebooks/README.md`](notebooks/README.md).
- **[`docs/coverage-assessment.md`](docs/coverage-assessment.md)** — what the coverage profiling measures,
  how the output maps to the matrix bands, and the validation anchors.
- **[`docs/epi-briefing.md`](docs/epi-briefing.md)** — presentation-ready status of every Epi question
  (answerable now vs needs-a-data-run vs external), with the meeting ask.
- **`docs/case_studies/`** — **worked examples** for two GSK cohorts (illustrative query snippets +
  feasibility commentary): [Case A — colon dMMR/MSI-H](docs/case_studies/A-colon-dMMR-MSI.md),
  [Case B — ovarian](docs/case_studies/B-ovarian.md) (LoT / platinum sensitivity). **Simplified demo ports** run
  as Databricks notebooks (`notebooks/caseA_colon_attrition_databricks.py`, `caseB_ovarian_lot_databricks.py`) —
  illustrative, not trusted analytic builds; the trusted end-to-end logic is the SQL appendix in
  `sql/reference_pipeline/`.
- **[`docs/variable-availability-matrix.md`](docs/variable-availability-matrix.md)** (+ `.csv`) —
  variable × data-layer matrix (off-the-shelf vs curation) + priority-tumour counts template.
- **[`docs/review-notes.md`](docs/review-notes.md)** — design-review disposition + readiness gate.
- **[`docs/optum-gsk-questions.md`](docs/optum-gsk-questions.md)** — the external confirmations that gate execution.
- **[`docs/feasibility-report-template.md`](docs/feasibility-report-template.md)** — output shell for when data lands.
- **`sql/reference_pipeline/`** *(optional appendix)* — the full, parameterized, end-to-end SQL behind the
  worked examples (6-cohort attrition, recursive LoT, value sets, teardown). Not the primary deliverable;
  keep for whoever eventually runs the analysis. See its `README.md`.

**What this is:** primarily **best-practice guidance + worked examples + runnable coverage profiling**
(the "use-cases for training" ask) — *how to use* each table and *what fraction of patients it covers*.
The Databricks notebooks in `notebooks/` are the runnable code to quantify coverage and fill the variables
matrix; they are **pointed at the deployed schema** `hive_metastore.clnprw_optum_mc_full_use` (cut `2025q4`,
from `Optum Data Dict/optum data location.pdf`). All referenced **table** names resolve — but they are **not
plug-and-play**: before a run you still need the SQL-warehouse `HOST`/`HTTP_PATH`, the clinical code lists, the
**biomarker LOINC value-set**, a few **column-level** `# TODO` checks (the notebooks run a **preflight** that
names any missing column), `onc_margin` keying, and — for the case studies — LoT/endpoint **threshold sign-off**.
The case-study notebooks are **simplified demo ports**, not trusted analytic builds. The full cohort-building
pipeline is preserved as an optional appendix (`sql/reference_pipeline/`).

**Status of the appendix pipeline: design-complete; execution scaffolded** (multiple review rounds — see
`docs/review-notes.md`). It is not runnable as-is: wire the per-cohort source placeholders, validate the
SQL dialect, and resolve the items in `docs/optum-gsk-questions.md` first.
>>>>> OPTUM-MC FILE: docs/best-practice-playbook.md
# Best-Practice Playbook — Optum Market Clarity / Enriched Oncology

> Answers GSK's "Best Practice Guidance / Epi needs" questions, grounded in the Optum
> reference docs. Every recommendation cites the tables/fields/codes in
> **`docs/data-model-reference.md`** (which in turn cites the source PDFs).
>
> **Reading rule for the whole asset:** Enriched Oncology has **high precision but moderate
> recall (0.6–0.8)**. What is present is usually correct; what is absent is frequently just
> *uncaptured*. **Absence ≠ negative.** Treat NLP-derived denominators as floors and corroborate
> across the structured EHR and claims layers.

---

## 0. The four data layers (use them deliberately)

| Layer | Strength | Use for |
|---|---|---|
| **Structured EHR** (Diagnosis, Labs, Procedure, Med Admin, Written Rx, Observations) | Coded, high recall where activity occurs | Cohort entry, labs/LOINC, procedures, structured meds |
| **Enriched Oncology — off-the-shelf** (`onc_*`) | Oncology depth from notes (stage, histology, biomarker, progression…) | Variables absent from structured fields |
| **Enriched Oncology — curation/derived** | Your business rules on top of the above | Custom LoT, neoadj/adj, platinum status, PFS |
| **Claims** (Market Clarity medical + Rx) | Cross-network continuity, fills/HCRU | Treatment completeness, continuity, cost |

The recurring best practice: **anchor in structured/coded data, enrich with NLP, reconcile with claims.**

---

## 1. Patient identification & cohort entry

**Question:** *"Patient identification: ICD-10 codes + histological confirmation?"*

**Recommended approach — index on coded diagnosis, confirm with histology:**
1. **Index** from the structured **Diagnosis** table using the **Cancer ICD-9/10 inclusion list** (manual appendix §4.8.4). Filter `diagnosis_cd_type LIKE 'ICD%'` and `diagnosis_status = 'Diagnosis of'` (exclude rule-outs). Require **≥2 diagnoses on separate encounters** to reduce rule-out contamination (manual §1.3: EHR codes are often used to "rule out").
2. **Confirm histology** by joining `onc_neoplasm_histology` on `PTID` and limiting `neoplasm_type` / `histology` to the target cancer (manual §4.2.2). Use `neoplasm_histology_key` to pull associated concepts.
3. **Establish the diagnosis date** from `neoplasm_dx_date`; fall back to earliest structured diagnosis date if missing (manual §4.3.2).

**Caveats**
- The NLP `neoplasm_histology` set **may not align** with the ICD codes — by design (unknown primary, multiple sites coded to one ICD, benign tumors). Always **limit to the target cancer** (manual §4.2.2).
- Histological confirmation via NLP is subject to recall (histology recall ≈ 0.82) — a missing histology is **not** evidence of its absence; cross-check the structured **Labs**/pathology where available.

---

## 2. Staging & severity — there is no single source of truth (combine tables)

**Question:** *"Staging (TNM, FIGO); severity/staging/surgery available in several tables — single source of truth — correlation between tables?"*

**Answer:** Optum **intentionally** spreads staging across tables and says to **use them in combination** — "valuable information from each table cannot necessarily be converted from one to the other" (manual §4.3.1; deck p.26). Do **not** pick one table. Use this **triangulation hierarchy**:

| Concept | Table.field | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Overall stage group (0–IV) | `onc_stage.stage`, `.stage_prefix` | `stage_prefix` = clinical vs pathological; NCCN-consistent |
| TNM | `onc_tnm` (`t/n/m` + prefix/suffix) | **AJCC edition not captured**; may overlap Stage table |
| Tumor characteristic | `onc_characteristic` (benign/in situ/malignant/metastatic) | corroborates advanced disease |
| Grade | `Grade.grade` + grading-systems (`SYSTEM_NAME`: Gleason/Nottingham/Oncotype Dx) | normalized 1–4 + narrative |
| Metastasis | `Metastasis_Location` + structured Diagnosis mets ICD | **distant only**; use ICD to confirm primary vs secondary |
| Performance status | `Evaluation_System` (ECOG/Karnofsky) | severity proxy |

**Precedence rule (recommended):** prefer **pathological** over clinical (`stage_prefix`/`t_prefix='p'`); take the **most advanced corroborated** stage on/near `neoplasm_dx_date`; resolve conflicts using the Diagnosis table mets codes and treatment pattern; **drop patients with unresolvable conflicts** (or apply a documented tie-break) (manual §4.3.3).

**FIGO — separate stage from grade (they are different variables):**
- **FIGO *stage*** (extent of disease, I–IV) is the gyn **staging** system → read from `onc_stage.stage` (the stage group) near `neoplasm_dx_date`; this is the patient characteristic GSK requests for ovarian.
- **FIGO *grade*** (tumour differentiation) is a **grading system** → read from the grading-systems / `Evaluation_System` table where `SYSTEM_NAME='FIGO'` (manual §4.3.1 cites "FIGO grade for ovarian").
Do not conflate them: a FIGO grade value must not be reported as FIGO stage. Confirm both against histology + characteristic.

**Stage at diagnosis vs restaging:** link stage concepts to `onc_neoplasm_histology` via `neoplasm_dx_date`; the data are longitudinal so later rows may be **restaging** — filter on temporal proximity to first diagnosis (manual §4.3.2).

---

## 3. Biomarkers (and whether to add LOINC)

**Question:** *"Biomarkers — how should these tables be used? Should we also consider LOINC codes?"*

**Recommended approach — always read TWO places:**
1. **Enriched Oncology Biomarker table** — `biomarker` (HGNC-normalized), `test_name` (IHC/FISH/Flow), `gene_characteristics` (exon/codon/V600E…), `numeric_result`, `narrative_result`, `biomarker_temporal_status`.
2. **Structured Labs table** — genetic/biomarker test results **also live here** (manual §4.1, §4.1.2; biomarker labs list in appendix §4.8.3). The Labs side recovers tests the NLP missed (recall) and provides result dates (coalesce order/collect/result dates for linkage). The structured `lab` table **does expose `LOINC`** (+ `TEST_NAME`), confirmed from the data dict — so key on LOINC plus the lab name/test/result fields; the still-open item is the **biomarker LOINC value-set** to filter with (appendix §4.8.3 biomarker-lab list), not whether LOINC exists. (The *claims* `clm_lab_results` table uses `LOINC_CD` instead.)

**Conflicts** (specimen differences, repeat tests, true change over time): review recent-date trend, check related markers, cross-reference Labs (manual §4.1.3). Some markers genuinely change (BCR-ABL, blast %).

**dMMR/MSI-H (Case Study A):** positive if **MSI-H** result OR a deleterious mutation/loss in **any** of **MLH1 / MSH2 / MSH6 / PMS2** (manual §4.1.3). Corroborate with structured Labs (MMR IHC / MSI PCR / NGS).

**PD-L1:** captured per panel with assay clone in `test_name` (22C3 / 28-8 / SP142); each marker on its own row.

---

## 4. HPV status derivation (RDQ 316079)

**Question:** *"HPV status in oropharyngeal cancer — <1% had a test in biomarker or LOINC tables. How to use / derive?"*

**Why it's <1%:** moderate NLP recall + HPV/p16 status living in **pathology reports** that may be outside captured notes — this is **undercapture, not true absence**. Do **not** treat untested as HPV-negative.

**Recommended derivation hierarchy (positive if any positive):**
1. **Biomarker table** — `biomarker` in {HPV, p16/CDKN2A} with `narrative_result` positive/negative (p16 IHC is the routine surrogate).
2. **Structured Labs** — HPV high-risk DNA/RNA ISH/PCR and p16 IHC results (via the confirmed **`lab.LOINC`** column + lab name/result fields; the §4.8.3 biomarker-lab list supplies the LOINC value-set to match).
3. **Diagnosis codes** — HPV-association codes (e.g., **B97.7**) and HPV-associated histology context.
4. (Forward-looking) AJCC 8th separates p16+ oropharyngeal — but **AJCC edition is not captured**, so do not rely on stage to infer HPV.

**Report capture as a floor**, stratify "HPV+ / HPV− / unknown", and quantify the **unknown** fraction explicitly. Flag to Optum whether additional pathology-report curation can raise HPV capture.

---

## 5. Smoking & alcohol status (RDQ 316079)

**Question:** *"~50% had smoking status (historical); alcohol had high missingness."*

**Key fact:** smoking/alcohol are **not in the `onc_*` oncology tables** — they are **social-history / SDS** concepts. Source them from:
- **NLP SDS** (and SDS Family) and **NLP Measurement/Observations** — narrative smoking/alcohol mentions with sentiment/negation.
- **Structured EHR Observations / social-history** fields.
- **Diagnosis codes** — tobacco: `Z87.891`, `F17.*`, `Z72.0`; alcohol: `F10.*`, `Z72.1`.

**Expectations:** smoking ≈ 50% (consistent with provider-activity-driven capture); alcohol materially worse (often too sparse to use, as GSK already found). Use **never/former/current** where derivable; otherwise treat as **ever/unknown** and report missingness rather than imputing negative.

---

## 6. Treatment, Line of Therapy, oral/neoadjuvant/adjuvant

**Questions:** *"LOT rules; oral therapies? maintenance? surgery? neoadjuvant? adjuvant?; treatment completeness — claims or EHR?"*

**Understand the supplied LoT table first (its limits drive everything):**
- `Line_of_Therapy` is built from the **structured Procedure table, IV antineoplastics only** — **excludes oral chemo and notes-derived meds** (manual §4.6).
- Regimen = unique IV drugs in **first 30 days**; line ends on **regimen change / 90-day gap / 90-day EHR inactivity / death** (manual §4.6.1).
- It **does not match cancer type to treatment** — a regimen on a cancer's row only means the patient was diagnosed before it started; **verify dx↔treatment yourself** (manual §4.6.2).

**Therefore, for GSK's needs, build a custom LoT (curation layer):**
1. **Drug exposure** from the **Oncology Medications** table — includes **oral chemo**, structured EHR, and claims (Med Services + Rx, PAID only), matched on `generic_name`/NDC/HCPCS (manual §4.7). Apply an **`action` hierarchy** — `administered`/`filled` = **confirmed** exposure; `started`/`is on` = **likely**; `prescribed` = **intent only** (use only if corroborated); `stopped`/`suspended` = **not** an exposure start. Prefer reliable dates (`date_from_note='Y'`, or `'N'`/`'N/A'` for structured rows); drop implied/relative-date rows for date-sensitive logic.
2. **Regimen/line construction** following Optum's algorithm windows (30-day regimen, 90-day gap) but **across oral + IV**.
3. **Neoadjuvant vs adjuvant** = derive from **timing relative to surgery**: identify the cancer-directed **surgery** date (Procedure CPT/ICD-procedure codes); systemic therapy **before** surgery → neoadjuvant, **after** → adjuvant (within a clinically sensible window). There is **no native neoadj/adj flag**.
4. **Maintenance** = continuation of a single agent after a defined induction regimen — derive from regimen composition + duration.
5. **Completeness — claims vs EHR:** EHR shows **ordering/administration within partnered providers**; **claims show fills and cross-network administration** (written Rx ≠ filled; discontinuation not recorded — manual §1.3/§5). Use **claims as the completeness backbone** and EHR/notes for clinical context; reconcile (see §9).

**Surgery identification:** structured **Procedure** (CPT/HCPCS/ICD-procedure) + corroborate with `Surgical_Margin` and `Tumor_Size` (extracted from surgical/path notes).

---

## 7. Outcomes — ORR, PFS, OS

**Question:** *"Outcomes assessment: PFS, ORR, OS (mortality linkage)."*

| Endpoint | How to derive | Caveat |
|---|---|---|
| **ORR** | `Treatment_Response.response` (complete/partial radiological response). Associate to a regimen by **stacking tables sorted by PTID + date** (manual §4.5) — the response table has **no drug names**. | Narrative radiological response, **not RECIST**; treatment field often null → recover from structured meds/procedures |
| **PFS** | Time from treatment start → first **progression/recurrence** (deployed table **`onc_progression`**; filter `progression IN ('progression','recurrence')`, `progression_temporal_status='current'`; event date = **`note_date`**) **or** death | Progression recall ≈ 0.63 (undercapture → PFS can be over-estimated). Triangulate with Stage change, new mets, Tumor Size trend, treatment switch (manual §4.4.2) |
| **OS** | Time from index → **death** (`patient.date_of_death`, **`YYYYMM`** + `deceased_indicator`) | **Source confirmed** (data dict). Open item is **completeness / lag / fitness for OS** + month-only granularity — validate on a slice |

**Progression as a composite:** combine the Progression table with stage progression, new metastasis (Metastasis Location + mets ICD), tumor-size trend (longitudinal in ~10% of the 6% with size), and treatment change — no single signal is complete.

---

## 8. SDS / NLP-derived elements from physician notes

**Question:** *"Working with SDS tables, NLP-derived elements from physician notes."*

- **SDS** tables capture signs/diseases/symptoms with **term, location, attribute, sentiment, temporal status** — crucial: **sentiment encodes negation** ("denies"), so filter on it (an SDS row is not automatically a positive finding).
- Use **SDS Family** separately (family history, e.g., hereditary risk) — do not mix with patient-level SDS.
- Comorbidities & **safety/background-event rates** (the JADE ask) come from **SDS + structured Diagnosis** together.
- Apply the global temporal/qualifier discipline: `temporal_status='current'`, `qualifier='actual'` for present findings (deck p.17 #5; manual §4.4.2).

---

## 9. Continuum of care, discordance, structured vs unstructured

**Continuity of care** *(IDN flag, claims linkage):*
- EHR only sees **partnered providers** (deck p.17 #1) → gaps when patients move networks. **Claims linkage** (medical + Rx) restores cross-network visibility.
- Gate longitudinal analyses on **`Claims_Continuous_Enrollment` / `Patient_Observable_Time`** so absence reflects true gaps, not lack of coverage.
- Use the **IDN flag** to identify integrated-delivery-network patients (more complete in-network capture) vs others — confirm the exact field with Optum.

**Discordance — EHR vs claims** *(directly testable):* compare concept presence/values across layers keyed on `PTID` (+ date window): e.g., treatment in claims but not EHR (cross-network), diagnosis in EHR but not claims. Report concordance rates per concept. (Case Study A's 6 source-definition cohorts are literally this analysis.)

**Discordance — table vs table:** quantify agreement between Stage vs TNM vs Characteristic, NLP histology vs ICD, Biomarker table vs Labs. Expect partial agreement; report κ/overlap, don't assume one is ground truth.

**Structured vs unstructured:** structured = higher recall where coded (dx, labs, procedures, fills); unstructured/NLP = depth (stage, histology, biomarker, response) but moderate recall. **Default to structured for cohort entry and counts; use NLP to enrich; reconcile with claims for completeness.**

---

## 10. Data quality, validation & cohort hygiene (cross-cutting)

- **Inclusion threshold** of any NLP concept: precision ≥ 0.80, recall ≥ 0.70 — but **per-concept recall varies** (size/mets/progression ≈ 0.6). Carry the concept's metrics into every feasibility statement (manual §4.8.1; deck p.44).
- **Temporal/assertion discipline everywhere:** filter `*_temporal_status` and `*_qualifier`; never trust raw `note_date` as event date.
- **De-duplication:** Optum removes repeated, less-complete patient+note_date rows but **keeps conflicting values** for you to adjudicate (manual §3.3).
- **Orphan/stand-alone records:** collapse stand-alone neoplasm↔histology only when one neoplasm exists that day (`neo_num=1`); use orphan stage for single-cancer patients only (manual §4.3.4, §4.8.2).
- **Validation plan:** for each variable, report capture %, source agreement, and a clinically-sensible distribution (Optum's own QC checks ECOG 0–5 ranges, staging plausibility — mirror this).

---

## 11. Tokenization

**Question:** *"Is tokenization possible? Which vendors?"*

**Not addressed anywhere in the provided documents.** Patient-level tokenization (to link Optum to external datasets) is typically done via **Datavant** tokens in this market, but this **cannot be confirmed from these materials** — it is an **open question for Optum** (see `PLAN.md §6`). Flag whether the licensed instance ships tokens and which vendor/keys.

# Case Study A — dMMR/MSI-H locally advanced colon cancer

**GSK ask (deck `optum mc.pdf` p.2):** build a 6-step cohort and run it across **6 data-source
definitions**, then *"comment on the feasibility of using these varying data sources to assess
real-world treatment effectiveness, treatment tolerability, and natural history outcomes (disease
progression, treatment patterns, HCRU, background rates of safety outcomes), considering
longitudinality, data completeness, accuracy, and the ability to validate data quality."*

Worked example queries are below (§1). A **simplified demo port** runs as a **Databricks notebook**,
`notebooks/caseA_colon_attrition_databricks.py` (the attrition table) — illustrative, with the source-layer
cohorts still a template; the trusted parameterized build is the appendix `sql/reference_pipeline/caseA_colon.sql`.
Field/code references: `docs/data-model-reference.md`, `docs/question-to-table-mapping.md`.

> ⚠️ **Assumptions flagged for confirmation** (PLAN §6): (1) the **a/b** split within cohorts 2 & 3
> is not defined on the slide — modeled as **broad** vs **strict-with-continuous-enrollment**;
> (2) all **code lists are illustrative** and need clinical sign-off; (3) "third-party claims" identity (3a/3b)
> to be confirmed with Optum.

---

## 1. Worked example queries (illustrative)

Short, readable snippets that show **how each concept is derived** — the point of the "use-cases for
training" ask. Table/column names are the logical ones from `docs/data-model-reference.md`; these are
**illustrative** (no cohort-switch, attrition, or dialect hardening — that's in the appendix).

```sql
-- (a) Cohort entry: colon-cancer patients (structured Diagnosis, ICD-10), >=2 dates
select ptid, min(diag_date) as first_dx_date
from diagnosis
where regexp_like(diagnosis_cd, '^C18|^C19|^C20')          -- colon / rectosigmoid / rectum
  and diagnosis_status = 'Diagnosis of'
group by ptid having count(distinct diag_date) >= 2;

-- (b) Colon-cancer surgery date (Procedure, colectomy CPT — illustrative list)
select ptid, min(proc_date) as surgery_date
from procedure
where proc_cd in ('44140','44145','44150','44204','44210')  -- colectomy
group by ptid;

-- (c) Stage T1-4 N+ or T4 N0 (Enriched Oncology TNM table)
select distinct ptid
from onc_tnm
where try_cast(regexp_extract(t,'[1-4]') as integer) between 1 and 4
  and ( try_cast(regexp_extract(n,'[0-9]') as integer) > 0                                  -- N+
     or (try_cast(regexp_extract(t,'[1-4]') as integer) = 4
         and try_cast(regexp_extract(n,'[0-9]') as integer) = 0) );                         -- T4N0

-- (d) dMMR / MSI-H (Enriched Oncology Biomarker table; structured-Lab cross-check is a TODO - see note)
--     Assay-specific result semantics: MSI -> high/unstable/positive; MMR-IHC -> loss/absent/deficient or a
--     gene mutation. Do NOT count MMR 'positive' (positive IHC staining = retained protein = proficient).
select distinct ptid
from onc_biomarker
where (upper(biomarker) like '%MSI%'
       and (lower(narrative_result) rlike 'high|unstable|positiv' or upper(biomarker) like '%MSI-H%'))
   or (regexp_like(upper(biomarker),'MLH1|MSH2|MSH6|PMS2|MMR')
       and (lower(narrative_result) rlike 'loss|lost|absent|deficien'
            or lower(gene_characteristics) rlike 'mutat|loss|deletion'));

-- (e) IO use (Oncology Medications / Procedure J-codes)
select distinct ptid
from onc_medications
where upper(generic_desc) in ('PEMBROLIZUMAB','NIVOLUMAB','DOSTARLIMAB')
  and action in ('administered','started','is on');
```

Chaining (a)–(e) with the timing rules (newly-dx ≤183 d before surgery; no distant mets before surgery;
IO after dx and before mets) gives the 6-step cohort. The appendix runs that chain across all six
data-source cohorts and produces the attrition table.

---

## 2. The six cohort / data-source definitions (columns of the attrition table)

| # | Definition | Data layers used | Modeled as |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | EHR only | structured EHR | notes-free; structured Dx/Proc/Labs/Med-Admin only |
| 2a | EHR + linked **Optum** Medical & Rx claims (broad) | EHR + Optum CLM | claims linkage, no enrollment gate |
| 2b | EHR + linked **Optum** Medical & Rx claims (strict) | EHR + Optum CLM | + continuous-enrollment / observable-time gate |
| 3a | EHR + linked **third-party** Medical & Rx claims (broad) | EHR + 3P CLM | **not assessable in Optum MC** (no third-party-claims asset in this schema) — the notebook renders `n/a` |
| 3b | EHR + linked **third-party** Medical & Rx claims (strict) | EHR + 3P CLM | **not assessable in Optum MC** (same) — needs a licensed third-party dataset |
| 4 | **Market Clarity Enriched Oncology** | EHR + Optum CLM + `onc_*` | full enrichment (notes-derived stage/biomarker/IO) |

The **point** of the design: each added layer should *recover* patients/criteria the leaner layers
miss. Cohort 4 (with NLP enrichment) should have the **highest capture** of stage, dMMR/MSI-H, and
IO; the EHR-only cohort the lowest. Reporting attrition side-by-side **is** the feasibility/discordance analysis.

---

## 3. The six attrition steps (rows) → tables, codes, logic

| Step | Criterion | Primary source | Logic | Capture by layer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | Colon-cancer **surgery** | `Procedure` (CPT/HCPCS/ICD-PCS colectomy) + colon ICD (C18–C20) | `surgery_date` = earliest qualifying colectomy | EHR ✅; claims add cross-network surgeries |
| **2** | **Newly diagnosed** colon cancer ≤183 d before surgery | `Diagnosis` colon ICD | first colon dx in `[surgery_date-183, surgery_date]`; **washout**: no colon dx before window | EHR ✅; claims improve "newly" (longer lookback) |
| **3** | Stage **T1-4 N+** or **T4 N0** | `onc_tnm` (T,N, keyed); else `Lymph_Node` (stand-alone → join PTID+date) | `(t in 1..4 and n>0) or (t=4 and n=0)`; N-missing → nodes>0. **Timing:** peri-operative window **[first_dx−30 d, surgery+90 d]** to include the post-op **pathological** stage; later notes = restaging (excluded). `has_pathologic` is reported, not used to select | **EO only** (cohorts 1-3 lack NLP stage → big drop) |
| **4** | **No DISTANT metastatic** dx before surgery | `flag_mets` = **C78–C79** ICD (distant) + keyed `Metastasis_Location` | exclude if `first_mets_date < surgery_date`. **Excludes C77 (regional nodes)** — N+ regional disease is *in-cohort* for locally advanced (see ⚠️ below) | EHR/claims dx ✅; EO mets adds distant sites |
| **5** | **dMMR+ or MSI-H** | `flag_dmmr_msi` (Biomarker MMR/MSI) + Labs *(spec/appendix; the notebook port currently uses **EO Biomarker only** — the structured-Lab arm needs the LOINC value-set, TODO)* | MSI-H (MSI high/unstable/positive) **OR** MLH1/MSH2/MSH6/PMS2 **defect** (loss/absent/deficient/mutation — **not** MMR "positive", which = retained/proficient) | **EO + Labs**; EHR-only weakest |
| **6** | **IO use** after dx, before metastatic dx | `Oncology_Medications` / Procedure J-codes (IO) | any IO in `(first_dx_date, first_mets_date)` | EO+claims best; EHR-only misses cross-network infusions |

**Key structural finding:** steps **3 (NLP stage)** and **5 (dMMR/MSI-H)** are the binding
constraints — they depend on Enriched Oncology + Labs. In the **EHR-only** and **claims-only**
cohorts these steps will attrit hardest (claims carry **no staging** and only partial biomarker
signal), which is exactly the feasibility story GSK wants quantified.

> ⚠️ **Two clinical correctness rules baked into the SQL (confirm with leads):**
> 1. **Metastatic exclusion = DISTANT disease only** (`C78–C79` + the distant-only
>    `Metastasis_Location` table). **`C77` (secondary in regional lymph nodes) is *not* an
>    exclusion** — locally advanced colon cancer is N+ by definition, so excluding regional nodal
>    codes would drop the target population.
> 2. **Stage timing** — the T/N criterion uses the **initial / peri-operative** stage over
>    `[first_dx − 30 d, surgery + 90 d]`, which captures **both** pre-op clinical staging **and** the
>    post-op **pathological** stage from the surgical specimen (the gold standard for a surgical
>    cohort). Later rows = **restaging**, excluded (manual §4.3.2). The window upper bound is an
>    open GSK item (`optum-gsk-questions.md` #17). `onc_tnm` is keyed to the tumour; `onc_lymph_node`
>    is **stand-alone** (joined on PTID + date, not the key).

---

## 4. Feasibility commentary template (to populate on data access)

Per data-source cohort, report against the GSK prompt:

- **Treatment effectiveness** — can we observe regimen → response/progression? (Treatment Response +
  Progression; IO exposure via meds/claims). EHR-only: clinical context but treatment incompleteness;
  claims: completeness but no response; EO: response narratives but moderate recall.
- **Treatment tolerability / safety background rates** — AEs/comorbidities from `Diagnosis` + `NLP_SDS`;
  HCRU/toxicity proxies from claims (ED/inpatient via `Claims_Inpatient_Confinement`).
- **Natural history** — disease progression (Progression + stage change + new mets), treatment patterns
  (custom LoT), HCRU (claims). Note progression recall ≈0.63 → undercapture.
- **Longitudinality** — gate on `Claims_Continuous_Enrollment`/`Patient_Observable_Time`; EHR-only limited
  to partnered-provider windows.
- **Completeness** — per-step capture % across the 6 cohorts (the attrition table itself).
- **Accuracy** — concordance of NLP stage/biomarker vs structured Labs/path; carry per-concept PPV.
- **Validate data quality** — distribution checks (stage plausibility, ECOG 0-5), source agreement,
  drop unresolvable conflicts (manual §4.3.3).

---

## 5. Overlap / discordance output

Beyond attrition Ns, produce **PTID-set overlap** across the 6 cohorts (Venn / UpSet) and, for the
populated steps, **concept-level concordance** (e.g., NLP stage vs structured; IO in claims vs EHR).
This answers both "discordance between EHR and claims" and "discordance between data tables."
>>>>> OPTUM-MC FILE: docs/case_studies/B-ovarian.md
# Case Study B — Ovarian cancer cohort, LoT & platinum sensitivity

**GSK ask (`optum mc.pdf` p.3–4):** counts/proportions and (where possible) patient characteristics for:
total OC; **by LoT** (neoadjuvant, adjuvant, 1L, 2L+); **2L+ stratified platinum-sensitive vs -resistant**;
each across the **same 6 data-source cohorts** as Case A; and *"note the overlap of patient data between
different sources (claims, EHR, enriched oncology) for each cohort."*

Worked example queries are in §0 below. A **simplified demo port** runs as a **Databricks notebook**,
`notebooks/caseB_ovarian_lot_databricks.py` (total / LoT / platinum strata, with LoT sessionised in pandas) —
a demonstration only (gap-based LoT, no maintenance split, enriched cohort only); the trusted parameterized
build is the appendix `sql/reference_pipeline/caseB_ovarian.sql`.

> ⚠️ Same assumption flags as Case A (a/b split, illustrative code lists, third-party-claims identity).
> The **platinum-sensitivity** and **neoadjuvant/adjuvant** logic here are **curation-layer** derivations —
> the supplied LoT table cannot produce them (IV-only, no oral, no neoadj/adj flag).

---

## 0. Worked example queries (illustrative)

Short snippets showing **how each ovarian concept is derived**. Illustrative only — the full
line-of-therapy construction and platinum-sensitivity logic are in the appendix pipeline.

```sql
-- (a) Total ovarian cancer cohort (ICD; histology as an enrichment check, not a gate)
select ptid, min(diag_date) as first_dx_date
from diagnosis
where regexp_like(diagnosis_cd, '^C56') and diagnosis_status = 'Diagnosis of'   -- ovary
group by ptid having count(distinct diag_date) >= 2;

-- (b) Ovarian drug exposure incl. ORAL (Oncology Medications unifies notes+EHR+claims)
select ptid, action_date, upper(generic_desc) as drug
from onc_medications
where upper(generic_desc) in ('CARBOPLATIN','CISPLATIN','PACLITAXEL',     -- platinum/taxane
                              'OLAPARIB','NIRAPARIB','BEVACIZUMAB')        -- + oral maintenance
  and action in ('administered','filled','started','is on');

-- (c) Neoadjuvant vs adjuvant = systemic-therapy timing vs primary cytoreductive surgery
--     therapy BEFORE surgery_date -> neoadjuvant; within ~180 d AFTER -> adjuvant (non-exclusive)

-- (d) Platinum sensitivity (2L+): platinum-free interval to first progression
--     last platinum administration -> first progression/recurrence;
--     > 183 days = platinum-sensitive, else platinum-resistant

-- (e) Patient characteristics (feasibility-rated)
select oc.ptid,
       fs.stage         as figo_stage,        -- onc_stage near diagnosis (FIGO stage, not grade)
       nh.histology,                          -- serous / mucinous / endometrioid / clear-cell
       ev.value         as ecog               -- Evaluation_System (ECOG / Karnofsky)
from total_oc oc
left join onc_stage             fs on fs.ptid = oc.ptid
left join onc_neoplasm_histology nh on nh.ptid = oc.ptid
left join evaluation_system     ev on ev.ptid = oc.ptid;
```

Steps (c)/(d) are written as comments because they are **multi-step derivations** (line-of-therapy
sessionization, platinum-free-interval timing) — the appendix implements them in full; the point here is
the *approach*, not a runnable build.

---

## 1. Cohort & strata

| Stratum | Definition | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **Total OC** | Ovarian ICD (C56.x) **≥2 encounters — all six cohorts**. NLP histology reported as a separate **`histology_confirmed`** enrichment metric (set only for the EO cohort), *not* a gate | `Diagnosis` (+ `onc_neoplasm_histology` flag) |
| **Neoadjuvant** | confirmed systemic therapy **before** primary surgery — **non-exclusive** | drug dates vs `surgery_date` |
| **Adjuvant** | confirmed systemic therapy **after** primary surgery — **non-exclusive** (a patient given chemo both before *and* after surgery is counted in both) | drug dates vs `surgery_date` |
| **1L** | first **non-maintenance** line | custom LoT |
| **2L+** | **≥2 non-maintenance** lines — maintenance (PARP/bev) is flagged separately and **does not** make a 1L patient "2L+" | custom LoT (`b_tx_lines`) |
| **2L+ platinum-sensitive** | first progression/recurrence **>183 d** after the platinum line preceding the 2nd line | custom LoT + Progression |
| **2L+ platinum-resistant** | first progression **≤183 d** after that platinum line | custom LoT + Progression |

> **Why Total OC changed:** an earlier version gated Total OC on EO histology, which made cohorts 1–3
> empty. Since the case study's purpose is to *compare* counts across data sources, Total OC is now
> **ICD-identified for every cohort**, with histology confirmation as an enrichment column.
>
> **Open (GSK):** the platinum-sensitivity **anchor** — modeled here as the last platinum dose of the
> platinum line *preceding the 2nd treatment line*; confirm vs "latest platinum exposure"
> (`docs/optum-gsk-questions.md` #11/#12).

---

## 2. Why LoT must be rebuilt (curation layer)

The supplied `Line_of_Therapy` table is **structured Procedure, IV antineoplastics only** and has **no
oral chemo and no neoadjuvant/adjuvant flag** (manual §4.6). Ovarian care is platinum/taxane +
**oral maintenance** (e.g., PARP inhibitors, bevacizumab) and is defined relative to **cytoreductive
surgery** — so:

1. **Exposure** from `Oncology_Medications` (oral + IV + claims; PAID claims only), matched on
   `generic_desc`/NDC/HCPCS, with the **action hierarchy** (administered/filled = confirmed;
   started/is on = likely; prescribed = intent only).
2. **Regimen/line** = unique agents in first 30 days; new line on regimen change / 90-day gap / death
   (Optum windows, applied across oral+IV).
3. **Neoadjuvant vs adjuvant** = first systemic-therapy date vs **primary cytoreductive surgery** date
   (`Procedure`): before → neoadjuvant, after → adjuvant.
4. **Platinum line end** = last administration date of the **platinum-containing line**.
5. **Platinum sensitivity** = interval from platinum-line end to first **progression/recurrence**
   (deployed table **`onc_progression`**; `progression IN ('progression','recurrence')`,
   `progression_temporal_status='current'`, **tied to ovarian via key**): **>183 days = sensitive,
   ≤183 days = resistant** (use a **day** threshold, not calendar-month `date_diff`; the event date is
   **`note_date`** — a *note-date proxy* for the event, so document that caveat).

> ⚙️ **The custom-LoT scan is implemented as a recursive SQL reference** in `sql/reference_pipeline/caseB_ovarian.sql`
> (`b_lines`): biosimilar canonicalization (applied **before** drug-class join), post-diagnosis window,
> 30-day regimen, line breaks on **90-day gap *and* a non-regimen drug after the window** (multi-switch
> via recursive walk), maintenance flagging, and **last-platinum-administration** anchoring. Platinum
> sensitivity is computed for **2L+ patients only**, from the last platinum dose to the **first progression
> after it** (>183 d sensitive). **Validate before production:** Trino/Athena recursion has a default depth
> (~10) that truncates long histories — raise `max_recursion_depth` or implement the LoT **procedurally**
> (Python/stored proc) where sessionization is unbounded. Treat as **validated-pending**, not engine-final.
>
> **Known limitation — continuation maintenance.** The line scan opens a new line on a 90-day gap or a
> *new* non-regimen drug. **Continuation** maintenance (e.g., bevacizumab carried over from an induction
> regimen after platinum/taxane are dropped — no new drug added) won't start a new line, so it can be
> missed as a distinct maintenance line. PARP started *after* induction is captured. Flag for refinement.

---

## 3. Patient characteristics (feasibility-rated)

| Characteristic | Source | Capture |
|---|---|---|
| Age at diagnosis | `Patient` + `neoplasm_dx_date` | ✅ |
| FIGO **stage** (extent I–IV) | `onc_stage.stage` near dx — **not** the FIGO *grade* in the grading table | 🟡 |
| Histology | `onc_neoplasm_histology.histology` (serous/mucinous/endometrioid/clear-cell) | 🟡 |
| Race / ethnicity | `Patient` | 🟡 |
| BMI | `NLP_Measurement` + `Observations` | 🟡 |
| ECOG | `Evaluation_System` | 🟡 |
| Primary cytoreductive surgery (Y/N) | `Procedure` (debulking/oophorectomy codes) + `Surgical_Margin`/`Tumor_Size` | ✅/🟡 |

Report each with capture % and source; per GSK's note, present **two rows** (off-the-shelf vs curated).

---

## 4. Source overlap (the blue-box ask)

For each stratum, compute **PTID-set overlap** across claims / EHR / enriched-oncology (and the 6
cohort definitions): how many OC patients are visible in each layer, and where strata depend on a
layer the leaner cohorts lack (FIGO/histology/maintenance → EO; fills/HCRU → claims). This quantifies
the marginal value of enrichment for ovarian RWE.
>>>>> OPTUM-MC FILE: docs/coverage-assessment.md
# Coverage assessment — "how can we use the tables, and what's the coverage?"

This is the runnable answer to GSK's **"Summary of the key data variables included in the database"**
slide (the captured / partially-captured / not-captured matrix, "one row for off-the-shelf oncology data
and another for curation data") and to the use-case coverage findings (HPV **<1%**, smoking **~50%**,
alcohol high-missingness). It measures, for every key variable, **what fraction of patients actually have
it populated**, by source layer — so you know what you can use.

## Code
The runnable version is the Databricks notebooks (see `notebooks/README.md`); they replaced the earlier
SQL scripts. Two notebooks, driven by the shared `notebooks/_config_tumours.py`:
- **`notebooks/coverage_optum_databricks.py`** — direct-variable coverage by source (EO off-the-shelf/curated
  plus selected structured-EHR and claims inputs), the off-the-shelf-vs-curated lift, and staging source
  coverage + overlap. Runs an all-EO overview plus one set of outputs per tumour. **Not** the full five-layer
  matrix: it does not produce the third-party-claims column or the EHR-vs-Optum-claims discordance (the schema
  has a single claims layer and no third-party asset — see `data-model-reference.md` §2.1).
- **`notebooks/epi_counts_optum_databricks.py`** — the presentation view: the ID funnel + one count per
  Epi question, per tumour.

They run in the house Databricks style (`run_sql` + pandas, Spark SQL), apply `MIN_CELL=11` small-cell
suppression, and write a CSV per output. Keyed variables (stage/TNM/grade/progression) count through the
target tumour's `neoplasm_histology_key`; per-patient tables (biomarker, ECOG, meds, etc.) join on `ptid`.

> Schema is mapped: the notebooks point at `hive_metastore.clnprw_optum_mc_full_use` (cut `2025q4`) and every
> referenced table resolves (see `docs/data-model-reference.md` §2.1). What remains is column-level — a few
> `# TODO` predicates listed in `notebooks/README.md`. Derived endpoints (neoadjuvant/adjuvant, maintenance,
> platinum sensitivity, PFS, OS, concordance) aren't profiled here — they're built from these inputs in a later pass.

## How to run
1. Fill in `HOST`/`HTTP_PATH` (the schema/cut are already set; bump `CUT` for a newer data cut).
2. Set the cohort in `notebooks/_config_tumours.py` — one entry per cancer. Coverage only means something
   against the right denominator: HPV "<1%" is over oropharyngeal patients, not everyone.
3. Run `coverage_optum_databricks.py` for the matrix, `epi_counts_optum_databricks.py` for the Epi counts.

## How the output fills the matrix
Each `(variable, source, pct_coverage)` row maps to one cell of
`docs/variable-availability-matrix.md`. Convert % to the colour bands GSK used, e.g.:

| pct_coverage | matrix band |
|---|---|
| ≥ ~70% | captured (green) |
| ~10–70% | partial (yellow) |
| < ~10% | sparse / derivable-only (orange) |
| 0% / table absent | not captured (red) |

The **off-the-shelf vs curation** rows from view A populate the slide's two requested rows directly:
the EO-only number is "off-the-shelf," the triangulated number is "curation."

## Validation anchors (sanity-check the numbers against the source docs)
- **Tumour size ≈ 6%** of patients (manual §4.4.1) — if the run returns far from this, check the
  table/field names.
- **HPV / p16 < 1%** off-the-shelf (RDQ 316079) — and the **curated** row should be materially higher
  (Labs + B97.7), which is the headline "how to use it" message.
- **Smoking ≈ 50%**, **alcohol** much lower (use-case doc) — these come from SDS/Observations, *not* the
  `onc_*` tables.

## How this answers the GSK questions
- *"What coverage / can we use these tables?"* → the coverage report, per variable, per source.
- *"Biomarkers — how to use, and LOINC?"* → view A shows the lift from adding **biomarker-specific**
  structured Labs (LOINC value-set / lab-name patterns — not every routine lab).
- *"Severity/staging in several tables — single source of truth?"* → view B shows no single table is
  complete (the union is what you use); view C gives the pairwise overlaps so you can judge correlation.
- *"Structured vs unstructured / off-the-shelf vs curation"* → the source column and view A (incl. the
  smoking/alcohol SDS-only vs SDS+obs+dx arms).

> Note: these are simple counts (`count(distinct ptid)`) — the right tool for the coverage question. The
> cohort-building logic (attrition, LoT, platinum strata) is the separate, optional `sql/reference_pipeline/`.
>>>>> OPTUM-MC FILE: docs/data-model-reference.md
# Optum Market Clarity + Enriched Oncology — Data-Model Reference

> Distilled from the two reference documents in `Optum Data Dict/`:
> - **`optum onco training decl.pdf`** — Optum "NLP & Enriched Oncology" training deck (46 slides)
> - **`optum enriched oncology manual.pdf`** — "Enriched Oncology User Guide" methodology manual (27 pp.)
>
> This file is the single factual reference that the playbook, mapping, case-study specs,
> and availability matrix all build on. **Traceability:** claims carry inline citations to the
> source — `deck p.N` = training deck slide, `manual §X` = user-guide section — so each statement
> can be checked against the original PDFs in `Optum Data Dict/`.

---

## 1. What the asset is

- **Optum Market Clarity** = de-identified, **linked EHR + administrative claims** (medical + pharmacy), joined at the patient level.
- **Enriched Oncology** = an **add-on** that uses proprietary **NLP/ML on unstructured provider notes** to surface oncology concepts (staging, histology, biomarkers, progression, LoT, etc.) that are *not* reliably present in structured EHR fields. It is **complementary to**, not a replacement for, the structured EHR/claims data.
- Scale: **~5.1M patients, ~4B clinical notes** (deck p.12). Notes dated **2007-01-01 → present**; refreshed **quarterly** (manual §1.2). Covers **solid tumors and hematologic cancers**.
- Intended use: longitudinal view from **index diagnosis → outcomes**, used **in conjunction with** structured data.

---

## 2. Linkage / keys (how everything joins)

| Key | Meaning | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| **PTID** | Patient ID — **the universal join key** | EHR, NLP, Enriched Oncology, claims |
| **ENCID** | Encounter ID | EHR/NLP encounter-level rows |
| **VISITID** / INDEX_VISITID / READMISSION_VISITID | Visit & visit-relationship keys | Visit, Visit Relationship |
| **PROVID / PROVIDER_PERFORM / PROVIDER_ORDER / PROV_UNIQUE** | Provider keys | EHR / claims provider tables |
| **PAT_PLANID** | Claims member/plan key | Market Clarity claims tables |
| **CONF_ID** | Inpatient confinement key | Claims confinement/services |
| **neoplasm_histology_key** | **Primary key of the Enriched Oncology concept model** — links a neoplasm/histology row to its stage, TNM, grade, characteristics, progression, metastasis, tumor size | Enriched Oncology `onc_*` tables (the **keyed** ones) |

> ⚠️ **Keyed vs stand-alone (drives cancer attribution).** Per manual §2.1, the core concepts
> *neoplasm* and *histology* carry `neoplasm_histology_key`, and so do **stage, TNM, characteristics,
> grade, metastasis location, tumour size, progression** — these can be attributed to a specific
> tumour via the key. But **biomarker, lymph node, and evaluation-system (ECOG/Karnofsky) tables are
> *stand-alone* — they have NO `neoplasm_histology_key`.** For these, attribute to the target cancer
> by **`PTID` + `note_date` proximity + a single-primary-cancer restriction + clinical judgment**, not
> by the key. This is critical for multi-primary patients (don't let another malignancy's biomarker/
> node qualify or exclude the wrong cohort).

**EHR ↔ Enriched Oncology join rule (deck p.45):** join on **`PTID` + Date**, and within oncology use **`neoplasm_histology_key`** to gather related concepts. Manual §4.8.2 confirms: *"use the primary key `neoplasm_histology_key` to retrieve related concepts from other tables."*

**Schema/version naming seen in the appendix SQL (manual §4.8.2):**
`oncology_202202_v1_20220615_prod.onc_*` and `market_clarity_panther_202202_v1_20220901_prod.diag` — i.e., versioned, date-stamped production schemas. **These are the manual's examples; the actual deployed location is below.**

### 2.1 Deployed location (this workspace)

> Source: `Optum Data Dict/optum data location.pdf` (Catalog Explorer screenshots) + the data dictionary.

- **Workspace host:** `adb-2026261073024158.18.azuredatabricks.net`
- **Catalog:** `hive_metastore`
- **Schema:** `clnprw_optum_mc_full_use` — **one** schema holding *both* the linked claims/EHR (Market Clarity) tables *and* the Enriched Oncology `onc_*` tables (not separate EO/MC schemas as the manual's examples imply).
- **Data cut:** `2025q4`. **Every** table is named `t_<name>_2025q4` (e.g. `t_onc_neoplasm_histology_2025q4`, `t_diagnosis_2025q4`). There are **52 tables**.
- The notebooks encode this as `def T(n): return f"hive_metastore.clnprw_optum_mc_full_use.t_{n}_2025q4"`; bump `CUT` for a newer cut.

**Deployed table names (52), by group** — the dictionary's short name (col. B) → the deployed `t_*_2025q4` table:

| Group | Deployed tables (`t_…_2025q4`) |
|---|---|
| Patient / demographics | `patient`, `pat_obs_time`, `patrep` |
| Claims | `clm_member_detail`, `clm_co_enrollment`, `clm_derived_enrollment`, `clm_inpat_confinement`, `clm_lab_results`, `clm_med_diagnosis`, `clm_med_procedure`, `clm_med_services`, `clm_provider`, `clm_rx` |
| Structured EHR | `diagnosis`, `procedure`, `encounter`, `enc_provider`, `provider`, `visit`, `vis_rel`, `observation`, `lab`, `microbiology`, `immunization`, `insurance`, `carearea`, `prescription`, `administrations`, `drug_rat`, `contraindications`, `biomarker` |
| NLP | `nlp_targeted`, `nlp_sds`, `nlp_sds_fam`, `nlp_measure` |
| Enriched Oncology (`onc_*`) | `onc_neoplasm_histology`, `onc_characteristic`, `onc_stage`, `onc_tnm`, `onc_grade`, `onc_evaluation_system`, `onc_metastatic_location`, `onc_tumor_size`, `onc_progression`, `onc_lymph_node`, `onc_margin`, `onc_biomarker`, `onc_medications`, `onc_line_of_therapy`, `onc_treatment_response`, `onc_histology_seer`, `onc_problem` |

⚠️ **Naming drift to watch:** the deployed names are the **expanded** forms, not the dictionary's abbreviations
(`t_clm_member_detail` not `clm_mbr_dtl`; `t_diagnosis` not `diag`; `t_procedure` not `proc`; `t_visit` not `vis`;
`t_observation` not `obs`; `t_onc_progression` not `onc_tumor_progression`; `t_onc_metastatic_location` not
`onc_metastasis_location`; `t_onc_margin` not `onc_surgical_margin`). The notebooks' `T()` calls use the deployed
names.

> **Single claims layer.** This schema has one claims layer (`clm_*` above). There is **no separate
> third-party-claims asset** here — so any analysis that wants an EHR-only vs Optum-claims vs third-party-claims
> split cannot get it from this schema alone. Case A's `3a`/`3b` (third-party) cohorts are therefore flagged
> **not-assessable and render `n/a`**; `2a`/`2b` (Optum-claims) still read the same structured tables as cohort 1
> until their claims UNION is wired (a TODO).

---

## 3. Table inventory

### 3.1 Structured EHR tables (deck p.4)
Patient · Provider · Visit · Encounter · Encounter Provider · Contraindications · Immunizations · Patient‑Reported Rx · Written Rx · Insurance · Care Area · **Diagnosis** · Observations · **Labs** · Microbiology · **Procedure** · **Medication Administration** · Visit Relationship

### 3.2 Market Clarity claims tables (deck p.4 — "MARKET CLARITY ONLY")
Claims Member Detail · **Patient Observable Time** · **Claims Continuous Enrollment** · Claims Med Diagnosis · Claims Lab Results · Claims Med Procedures · **Claims Rx** · Claims Inpatient Confinement · **Claims Med Services** · Claims Provider

### 3.3 General NLP tables (deck p.4, p.6–10)
NLP Targeted · NLP SDS · **NLP SDS Family** · NLP Measurement · NLP Biomarker
- **SDS** = Signs, Diseases & Symptoms (term, location, attribute, sentiment/negation, timing, severity, extent, duration, change, quality). Individual- and family-level in separate tables.
- **Measurement** = e.g. waist size 92 cm (type / value / detail / note-section).
- ⚠️ **Smoking / alcohol / social history live here (SDS / Observations / Measurement), NOT in the `onc_*` oncology tables.**

### 3.4 Enriched Oncology tables (deck p.19–31, p.45; manual §4)
| Table (logical / physical) | Key columns |
|---|---|
| **Neoplasm & Histology** (`onc_neoplasm_histology`) | `neoplasm_histology_key`, `neoplasm_type`, `neoplasm_qualifier` (actual/absent/NA), `neoplasm_temporal_status` (current/history), `direction`, `histology`, `histology_qualifier`, `histology_temporal_status`, `neoplasm_dx_date` |
| **Characteristics** (`onc_characteristic`) | `neoplasm_characteristic` (benign/in situ/malignant/metastatic), `*_char_temporal_status`, `histology_characteristic` |
| **Stage** (`onc_stage`) | `stage_prefix` (clinical/pathological), `stage` (0–IV incl. sub-stages like 3c; NCCN-consistent), `source_id` |
| **TNM** (`onc_tnm`) | `stage_prefix`, `t_prefix/t/t_suffix`, `n_prefix/n/n_suffix`, `m_prefix/m/m_suffix` (prefix codes a/c/p/r/u/y/rp/yc/yp; suffix m/f/mi/i+/sn). ⚠️ **AJCC 7th vs 8th edition is NOT extracted.** May overlap Stage table. |
| **Grade** (`Grade`) | `grade` (normalized: III/3/G3 → "3"; narrative → well/moderately/poorly differentiated) |
| **Grading systems / Grade Characteristics** | `SYSTEM_NAME` (**Gleason, Nottingham, Oncotype Dx**, Recurrence), `GRADE_PRIMARY/SECONDARY/TERTIARY/TUBULAR/NUCLEAR/MITOTIC`, `RESULT_NARRATIVE`, `RESULT_NUMERIC`, `TEMP_STATUS` |
| **Evaluation System** (`Evaluation_System`) | performance status — **ECOG (0–5), Karnofsky**, etc. ("24 evaluation systems") |
| **Metastasis Location** | `metastasis_location`, `mets_qualifier`, `mets_temporal_status`, `metastasis_dx_date`, `metastasis_dx_date_type`. ⚠️ **distant sites only** — excludes local & lymph-node invasion |
| **Tumor Size** (`onc_tumor_size`) | `tumor_size_1/2/3`, `tumor_size_unit_1/2/3` (mm/cm). ⚠️ **only ~6% of patients**; ~10% of those longitudinal |
| **Tumor Progression** (deployed **`onc_progression`**; manual label `onc_tumor_progression`) | `progression` (complete remission / partial remission / stable disease / no progression / progression / recurrence), `progression_temporal_status` (current/history), **`note_date`** (the event date — there is no `progression_first_note` column), `neoplasm_histology_key` |
| **Lymph Node** (`Lymph_Node`) | `lymph_narrative`, `lymph_numeric` (# positive), `lymph_numeric_scale` (# examined) |
| **Surgical Margin** | `margin_distance`, `margin_distance_unit`, `margin_narrative` (positive/negative; positive has no distance) |
| **Biomarkers** | `biomarker` (HGNC-normalized), `test_name` (IHC/FISH/Flow…), `gene_characteristics` (exon/codon/V600E…), `numeric_result`, `narrative_result` (positive/negative/equivocal/amplified…), `modifier_narrative`, `biomarker_temporal_status` |
| **Treatment Response** | `treatment` (often null), `response` (e.g. "partial radiological response", "complete response"). ⚠️ **no drug/procedure names** here |
| **Line of Therapy** (LoT / Regimen) | `multi_cancer`, `cancer_type`, `regimen_name`, `lot` (1,2,…), `initiation`/end dates, `num_administrations`, `avg/min/max_interval`, `drugs`. ⚠️ **structured Procedure table only; IV antineoplastics only** |
| **Oncology Medications** | `drug_name`, `generic_desc`, `drug_class`, `action` (started/stopped/is on/administered/filled/prescribed/suspended), `action_date`, `date_from_note` (Y/N), `strength`, `frequency`, `amount`, `route`, `source`. ✅ **includes oral chemo + structured + claims meds** |
| **Problem** | `PTID`, `note_date`, `problem`, `qualifier` (actual/absent) |

> **Physical names — now confirmed against the deployed catalog (§2.1).** Every EO table the notebooks use
> exists in `clnprw_optum_mc_full_use` as `t_<name>_2025q4`: `onc_neoplasm_histology`, `onc_characteristic`,
> `onc_stage`, `onc_tnm`, `onc_grade`, `onc_evaluation_system`, `onc_metastatic_location`, `onc_tumor_size`,
> `onc_progression`, `onc_lymph_node`, `onc_margin`, `onc_biomarker`, `onc_treatment_response`,
> `onc_line_of_therapy` (+ `onc_histology_seer`, `onc_problem`). Note the deployed names differ from the
> manual's logical labels: progression is **`onc_progression`** (not `onc_tumor_progression`), distant mets is
> **`onc_metastatic_location`** (not `onc_metastasis_location`), and surgical margin is **`onc_margin`** (not
> `onc_surgical_margin`). Keyed tables (stage/TNM/characteristic/grade/metastasis/tumor_size/progression — and
> margin, **keying not stated in §2.1, treated as keyed pending confirmation**) are reached via
> `neoplasm_histology_key` through `onc_neoplasm_histology`. **Stand-alone** tables — **biomarker, lymph_node,
> evaluation_system** (per §2.1), plus treatment_response, line_of_therapy, medications — join on `ptid`
> (attribute to the target cancer by `ptid` + date for multi-primary patients). What still needs an instance
> check is **column-level**, not table-level (a handful of `# TODO` predicates in the notebooks).

## 4. Code systems & external references

| Domain | System(s) |
|---|---|
| Diagnosis | **ICD‑9 / ICD‑10** (`diagnosis_cd`, `diagnosis_cd_type LIKE 'ICD%'`, `diagnosis_status='Diagnosis of'`). Appendix §4.8.4 = Cancer ICD‑9/10 inclusion list |
| Procedures | **CPT, HCPCS** (incl. J‑codes for infused chemo) |
| Medications | **NDC, HCPCS**, generic‑name matching; claims Rx filtered to **PAID** transactions |
| Staging | **TNM**, AJCC (edition *not* captured), stage groups 0–IV; **NCCN** guidelines for stage/regimen consistency |
| Grading | **Gleason** (prostate), **Nottingham** (breast), **FIGO *grade*** (gyn), **Oncotype Dx** — in the grading-systems / `Evaluation_System` table |
| Staging (gyn) | **FIGO *stage*** (extent I–IV) → in `onc_stage.stage`, **not** the grading table. Keep FIGO *stage* (extent) separate from FIGO *grade* (differentiation). |
| Performance status | **ECOG (0–5), Karnofsky** |
| Biomarker normalization | **UMLS**, **HGNC** gene names, **NCCN biomarker compendium**; assays IHC / FISH / Flow Cytometry; PD‑L1 clones 22C3 / 28‑8 / SP142 |
| Labs | Biomarker results **also** in structured **Labs** table — appendix §4.8.3 lists biomarker labs (LOINC‑type lab content lives here) |

> ⚠️ **RxNorm, SNOMED, OMOP** are **not named** in the slides/manual. `neoplasm_histology_key` is an internal concept key. **LOINC is confirmed**: the structured `lab` table has a `LOINC` column (the claims `clm_lab_results` table uses `LOINC_CD`) — so the only open LOINC item is the **biomarker LOINC value-set** to match on, not availability/mapping in general.

---

## 5. NLP model performance (deck p.44; manual §4.8.1)

Inclusion threshold for any concept: **precision (PPV) ≥ 0.80 AND recall (sensitivity) ≥ 0.70.**
Definitions: Precision = TP/(TP+FP); Recall = TP/(TP+FN); F1 = harmonic mean.

| Concept | Precision | Recall | F1 | Support |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Neoplasm | 0.97 | 0.79 | 0.87 | 2106 |
| Histology | 0.94 | 0.82 | 0.88 | 1128 |
| Grade | 0.97 | 0.81 | 0.88 | 220 |
| Stage TNM | 0.96 | 0.81 | 0.88 | 399 |
| Stage value | 0.99 | 0.81 | 0.89 | 699 |
| Stage prefix | 0.91 | 0.92 | 0.91 | 125 |
| Tumor status | 0.94 | 0.87 | 0.91 | 845 |
| **Tumor size** | 0.85 | **0.61** | 0.71 | 64 |
| **Metastasis** | 0.89 | **0.60** | 0.72 | 282 |
| **Tumor progression** | 0.90 | **0.63** | 0.74 | 283 |

> **Read this as:** high precision (what's there is usually right) but **moderate recall (0.6–0.8) → systematic UNDERcapture**, worst for size/metastasis/progression. This is the root cause of GSK's "<1% HPV" and "50% smoking" observations: absence ≠ negative.

---

## 6. Methodology rules & caveats (the "best practice" source material)

**Temporal & assertion logic**
- **Note date ≠ event date.** Always use `*_temporal_status` (current/history) and `*_qualifier` (actual/absent/NA) — not raw note dates (deck p.17 #5).
- For solid-tumor progression, filter `progression_temporal_status='current'`, `neoplasm_qualifier='actual'`, `neoplasm_temporal_status='current'` (manual §4.4.2).

**Conflicts, duplicates, orphans**
- Optum does **not** reconcile conflicting/repeating *values* (intentional — leaves control to user), but **does** delete patient+note_date rows that only repeat less-complete info (manual §3.3).
- Conflicting stage → corroborate with Diagnosis (mets codes) / TNM; **drop patient if unresolvable**, or build your own rule (manual §4.3.3).
- **Orphan** stage/histology (no `neoplasm_histology_key`): usable for single-cancer patients; collapse stand-alone neoplasm + histology when only one neoplasm exists that day (`neo_num=1`) — SQL in §4.8.2.

**Staging — there is no single source of truth (answers a GSK question directly)**
- Stage/severity is spread across **Stage, TNM, Characteristics, Grade, Metastasis Location, Evaluation System** + structured Diagnosis/Procedure. Optum says **use them in combination** — "valuable information from each table cannot necessarily be converted from one to the other" (manual §4.3.1, deck p.26). Establish a **precedence hierarchy** (e.g., pathological > clinical; corroborate with mets ICD).
- **Stage at diagnosis:** link via `neoplasm_dx_date`; if missing, earliest `note_date` near structured first-dx date (manual §4.3.2).

**Biomarkers (answers a GSK question directly)**
- Biomarker data live in **both** the Enriched Oncology Biomarker table **and** the structured **Labs** table — **check both** (manual §4.1, §4.1.2).
- **dMMR/MSI‑H** is inferable from a mutation in any of **MLH1 / MSH2 / MSH6 / PMS2** (manual §4.1.3) — directly relevant to Case Study A.
- Conflicts arise from specimen/test differences or true change over time (BCR‑ABL, blast %); resolve via trend, related markers, Labs cross-reference.

**Line of Therapy (critical limitation for GSK's LoT/oral/neoadj questions)**
- LoT table = **structured Procedure, IV antineoplastics only**; **excludes oral chemo and notes-derived meds** (manual §4.6).
- Algorithm: regimen = unique IV drugs in **first 30 days**; line ends on **regimen change / 90-day gap / 90-day EHR inactivity / death**; compared to **NCCN** regimens (manual §4.6.1).
- ⚠️ Algorithm **does not match cancer type to treatment** — a regimen on a cancer's row only means the patient was diagnosed before it started; **verify dx↔treatment independently** (manual §4.6.2).
- **No native neoadjuvant/adjuvant flag** — must be derived from drug timing relative to **surgery** (Procedure table).

**Oncology Medications (use this for oral therapy / maintenance)**
- Combines NLP notes + structured EHR (Prescription, Med Admin, Procedure) + claims (Med Services, Rx — PAID only). Matched by `generic_name` / NDC / HCPCS. Oncology/PCP provider notes prioritized (manual §4.7).

**Outcomes**
- **ORR** ≈ Treatment Response table (radiological response). **PFS** = derive from Progression table + treatment dates (no native PFS field). **OS** = the **`patient`** table carries `date_of_death` (**`YYYYMM`**, so month-grained not day-grained) plus a `deceased_indicator` flag (1/0, from SS Death Index or provider-reported) — that is the mortality source. ⚠️ month-only death date limits OS to month resolution.

**EHR limitations (deck p.17; manual §1.3, §5)**
1. Only **partnered providers** are visible — network changes break longitudinality (claims help fill gaps).
2. Data is **provider-activity-driven** — capture varies by concept (biomarker << metastatic location).
3. Repeating/incomplete/conflicting notes are expected and **not reconciled**.
4. **High precision ≠ 100% accuracy** — dropping a patient is sometimes correct.
5. Written Rx **≠ filled**; discontinuation often **not recorded**; dx codes used to **rule-out**; samples **location-specific** (generalizability).

---

## 7. Reusable assets in the manual appendix (§4.8)
- **§4.8.1** NLP performance metric definitions + table (above).
- **§4.8.2** Worked **SQL**: build cancer cohort from Diagnosis → join `onc_neoplasm_histology` → pull characteristics/stage/TNM via `neoplasm_histology_key`; filter NSCLC; collapse stand-alone neoplasm↔histology; tumor-size & progression queries. **Directly adaptable to the case-study specs.**
- **§4.8.3** Biomarker **labs list** (for the Labs cross-reference).
- **§4.8.4** **Cancer ICD‑9 / ICD‑10 inclusion list** (the patient-identification backbone).
